- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of proxy

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A proxy is valid only at the meeting in respect of
  which it is given or at a continuation of the meeting after an
  adjournment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of proxy

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A shareholder may revoke a proxy

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by depositing an instrument in writing executed by
    the shareholder or by the shareholder's attorney authorized in
    writing to do so

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at the head office of the association at any time
      up to and including the last business day before the day of a
      meeting, or a continuation of the meeting after an adjournment, at
      which the proxy is to be used, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with the chairperson of the meeting on the day
      of the meeting or a continuation of the meeting after an
      adjournment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other manner permitted by law.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deposit of proxies

**[[166.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.03 .sectionLabel}** The directors may
specify, in a notice calling a meeting of shareholders or a continuation
of a meeting of shareholders after an adjournment, a time before which
executed forms of proxy to be used at the meeting or the continued
meeting must be deposited with the association or its transfer agent.
The time specified may not be more than forty-eight hours, excluding
Saturdays and holidays, before the meeting or the continued meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory solicitation

- **[[166.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 148(2) and
  subsection (2), the management of an association shall, concurrently
  with giving notice of a meeting of shareholders, send a form of proxy
  in prescribed form to each shareholder entitled to receive notice of
  the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The management of an association is not required to
  send a form of proxy under subsection (1) if the association

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not a distributing association; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has 50 or fewer shareholders who are entitled to
    vote at a meeting, two or more joint holders of a share being
    counted as one shareholder.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
- 2005, c. 54, s. 167
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-166.04-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 166.04"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Soliciting proxies

- **[[166.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person shall not solicit proxies
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of solicitation by or on behalf of the
    management of an association, a management proxy circular in
    prescribed form, either as an appendix to, or as a separate document
    accompanying, the notice of the meeting is sent to the auditor of
    the association and to each shareholder whose proxy is solicited;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other solicitation, a
    dissident's proxy circular in prescribed form stating the purposes
    of the solicitation is sent to the auditor of the association, to
    each shareholder whose proxy is solicited and to the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Superintendent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who sends a management proxy circular or
  dissident's proxy circular shall at the same time file with the
  Superintendent

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a management proxy circular, a copy
    of it together with a copy of the notice of meeting, form of proxy
    and any other documents for use in connection with the meeting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a dissident's proxy circular, a copy
    of it together with a copy of the form of proxy and any other
    documents for use in connection with the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption by Superintendent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On the application of an interested person, the
  Superintendent may, on any terms that the Superintendent thinks fit,
  exempt the person from any of the requirements of subsection (1) and
  section 166.04, and the exemption may be given retroactive effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reporting exemptions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Superintendent shall set out in a periodical
  available to the public the particulars of each exemption granted
  under subsection (3) together with the reasons for the exemption.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance at meeting

- **[[166.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.06
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who solicits a proxy and is
  appointed proxyholder shall attend in person or cause an alternate
  proxyholder to attend every meeting in respect of which the proxy is
  valid, and the proxyholder or alternate proxyholder shall comply with
  the directions of the shareholder who executed the form of proxy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of proxyholder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A proxyholder or an alternate proxyholder has the
  same rights as the appointing shareholder to speak at a meeting of
  shareholders in respect of any matter, to vote by way of ballot at the
  meeting and, except where a proxyholder or an alternate proxyholder
  has conflicting instructions from more than one shareholder, to vote
  at the meeting in respect of any matter by way of a show of hands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vote by show of hands

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) and (2) and unless a
  shareholder or proxyholder demands a ballot, if the chairperson of a
  meeting of shareholders declares to the meeting that, if a ballot were
  conducted, the total number of votes attached to shares represented at
  the meeting by proxy required to be voted against what, to the
  knowledge of the chairperson, would be the decision of the meeting on
  a matter or group of matters is less than 5% of all the votes that
  might be cast by shareholders in person or by proxy,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the chairperson may conduct the vote in respect of
    that matter or group of matters by way of a show of hands; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a proxyholder or alternate proxyholder may vote in
    respect of that matter or group of matters by way of a show of
    hands.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
- 2005, c. 54, s. 169
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-166.06-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 166.06"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of intermediary

- **[[166.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Shares of an association that are
  registered in the name of an intermediary or an intermediary's nominee
  and not beneficially owned by the intermediary may not be voted unless
  the intermediary sends to the beneficial owner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the notice of the meeting, annual
    statement, management proxy circular and dissident's proxy circular
    and any other documents, other than the form of proxy, that were
    sent to shareholders by or on behalf of any person for use in
    connection with the meeting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a written request for voting instructions except if
    the intermediary has already received written voting instructions
    from the beneficial owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When documents to be sent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The intermediary shall send the documents referred to
  in subsection (1) without delay after they receive the documents
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on voting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An intermediary or a proxyholder appointed by them
  may not vote shares that the intermediary does not beneficially own
  and that are registered in the name of the intermediary or their
  nominee unless the intermediary or proxyholder, as the case may be,
  receives written voting instructions from the beneficial owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person by or on behalf of whom a solicitation is
  made shall on request and without delay provide the intermediary, at
  the person's expense, with the necessary number of copies of the
  documents referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions to intermediary

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The intermediary shall vote or appoint a proxyholder
  to vote in accordance with any written voting instructions received
  from the beneficial owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Beneficial owner as proxyholder

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a beneficial owner so requests and provides an
  intermediary with the appropriate documentation, the intermediary
  shall appoint the beneficial owner or a nominee of the beneficial
  owner as proxyholder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of intermediary's failure to
  comply

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The failure of an intermediary to comply with any of
  subsections (1) to (6) does not render void any meeting of
  shareholders or any action taken at the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intermediary may not vote

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Part gives an intermediary the right
  to vote shares that they are otherwise prohibited from voting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
- 2005, c. 54, s. 170
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-166.07-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 166.07"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

**[[166.071]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.071 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations respecting the conditions under which an
association is exempt from any of the requirements of sections 166.02 to
166.07.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 54, s. 170
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restraining order

- **[[166.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.08
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a form of proxy, management proxy
  circular or dissident's proxy circular contains an untrue statement of
  a material fact or omits to state a material fact that is required to
  be contained in it or that is necessary to make a statement contained
  in it not misleading in light of the circumstances in which the
  statement is made, an interested person or the Superintendent may
  apply to a court and the court may make any order it thinks fit
  including,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order restraining the solicitation or the
    holding of the meeting, or restraining any person from implementing
    or acting on a resolution passed at the meeting, to which the form
    of proxy, management proxy circular or dissident's proxy circular
    relates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order requiring correction of any form of proxy
    or proxy circular and a further solicitation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order adjourning the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person other than the Superintendent is an
  applicant under subsection (1), the applicant shall give notice of the
  application to the Superintendent and the Superintendent is entitled
  to appear and to be heard in person or by counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 120
:::

### [Directors and Officers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-98246 .Subheading}

#### [Duties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98247 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to manage

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, the directors
  of an association shall manage or supervise the management of the
  business and affairs of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific duties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Without limiting the generality of subsection (1),
  the directors of an association shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish an audit committee to perform the duties
    referred to in subsections 199(3) and (4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish a conduct review committee to perform the
    duties referred to in subsection 200(3);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish procedures to resolve conflicts of
    interest, including techniques for the identification of potential
    conflict situations and for restricting the use of confidential
    information;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} designate a committee of the board of directors to
    monitor the procedures referred to in paragraph (c);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} establish investment and lending policies,
    standards and procedures in accordance with section 387;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a retail association, establish
    procedures to provide disclosure of information to customers of the
    association that is required to be disclosed by this Act and for
    dealing with complaints as required by section 385.22; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} designate a committee of the board of directors to
    monitor the procedures referred to in paragraph (f) and satisfy
    itself that they are being adhered to by the retail association.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 167
- 2001, c. 9, s. 275
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of care

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every director and officer of an
  association in exercising any of the powers of a director or an
  officer and discharging any of the duties of a director or an officer
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} act honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the association; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise the care, diligence and skill that a
    reasonably prudent person would exercise in comparable
    circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to comply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every director, officer and employee of an
  association shall comply with this Act, the regulations, the
  association's incorporating instrument and the by-laws of the
  association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No exculpation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No provision in any contract, in any resolution or in
  the by-laws of an association relieves any director, officer or
  employee of the association from the duty to act in accordance with
  this Act and the regulations or relieves a director, officer or
  employee from liability for a breach thereof.

#### [Qualification and Number --- Directors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98271 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum number of directors

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  association shall have at least seven directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residency requirement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The majority of the directors of an association must
  be, at the time of each director's election or appointment, resident
  Canadians.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 169
- 2001, c. 9, s. 276
- 2007, c. 6, s. 150
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-169-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 169"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disqualified persons

**[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** The following persons
are disqualified from being directors of an association:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who is less than eighteen years of age;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who is of unsound mind and has been so found
  by a court in Canada or elsewhere;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who has the status of a bankrupt;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person who is not a natural person;

- [(e) and (f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1994, c. 47, s. 51\]]{.Repealed}

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a minister of Her Majesty in right of Canada or in
  right of a province; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} a person who is an agent or employee of the
  government of a foreign country or any political subdivision thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 170
- 1994, c. 47, s. 51
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit on directors

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** No more than 15 per
cent of the directors of an association may, at each director's election
or appointment, be employees of the association or a subsidiary of the
association.

#### [Election and Tenure --- Directors]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98292 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of directors

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 169(1), the
  members of an association shall, by by-law, determine the number of
  directors or the minimum and maximum number of directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of by-law

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A by-law made pursuant to subsection (1) shall set
  out the manner in which directors are nominated, appointed or elected,
  but no by-law that decreases the number of directors shortens the term
  of an incumbent director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election at annual meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A by-law made pursuant to subsection (1) that
  provides for a minimum and maximum number of directors may provide
  that the number of directors to be appointed or elected at any annual
  meeting be such number as is fixed by the directors prior to the
  annual meeting.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election or appointment as director

**[[172.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.1 .sectionLabel}** The election or
appointment of a person as a director is subject to the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person was present at the meeting when the
  election or appointment took place and did not refuse to hold office
  as a director; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person was not present at the meeting when the
  election or appointment took place but

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} consented in writing to hold office as a director
    before the election or appointment or within 10 days after it, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} acted as a director after the election or
    appointment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 54, s. 171
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting by shareholders

**[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** Where shareholders of
an association are entitled to elect one or more directors of an
association, no more than one third of the directors may be elected by
the shareholders.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of directors

- **[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  association may, by by-law, provide that the directors be appointed or
  elected for terms of one, two or three years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of one, two or three years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director appointed or elected for a term of one,
  two or three years holds office until the close of the first, second
  or third annual meeting following the appointment or election of the
  director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No stated term

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director who is not appointed or elected for an
  expressly stated term of office ceases to hold office at the close of
  the next annual meeting following the appointment or election of the
  director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} It is not necessary that all directors appointed or
  elected at a meeting hold office for the same term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a by-law of an association provides that the
  directors be appointed or elected for a term of two or three years, it
  may also provide that the term of office of each director be for the
  whole of that term, or that, as nearly as may be, one half of the
  directors retire each year if the term is two years, and that one
  third of the directors retire each year if the term is three years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition requirements

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a director of an association is appointed or
  elected for a term of more than one year, the association shall comply
  with subsection 169(2) and section 171 at each annual meeting during
  the director's term of office as if that director were appointed or
  elected on that date.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determining election of directors

- **[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  persons, to the number authorized to be elected, who receive the
  greatest number of votes at an election of directors of an association
  shall be directors thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, at any election of directors referred to in
  subsection (1), two or more persons receive an equal number of votes
  and there are not sufficient vacancies remaining to enable all the
  persons receiving an equal number of votes to be elected, the
  directors who receive a greater number of votes or the majority of
  them shall, in order to complete the full number of directors to be
  elected, determine which of the persons so receiving an equal number
  of votes are to be elected.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment or re-election of
directors

**[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** A director who has
completed a term of office is, if otherwise qualified, eligible for
re-appointment or re-election.

#### [Director Vacancies]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98330 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Void election or appointment

- **[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  immediately after the time of any purported election or appointment of
  directors, the board of directors would fail to comply with subsection
  169(2) or section 171, the purported election or appointment of all
  persons purported to be elected or appointed at that time is void
  unless the directors, within forty-five days after the discovery of
  the non-compliance, develop a plan, approved by the Superintendent, to
  rectify the non-compliance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to appoint or elect minimum

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, at the close of a meeting of members or
  shareholders of an association, the members or shareholders have
  failed to appoint or elect the number or minimum number of directors
  required by this Act or the by-laws of the association, the purported
  appointment or election of directors at the meeting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is valid if the directors purported to be appointed
    or elected and those incumbent directors, if any, whose terms did
    not expire at the close of the meeting, together constitute a
    quorum; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is void if the directors purported to be appointed
    or elected and those incumbent directors, if any, whose terms did
    not expire at the close of the meeting, together do not constitute a
    quorum.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 177
- 1997, c. 15, s. 121
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors where appointment or elections
incomplete or void

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsections 174(2)
  and (3) and paragraph 179(1)(a), if section 177 applies at the close
  of any meeting of members or shareholders of an association, the board
  of directors shall, until such time as their successors are appointed
  or elected, consist solely of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where paragraph 177(2)(a) applies, the directors
    referred to in that paragraph; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where subsection 177(1) or paragraph 177(2)(b)
    applies, those persons who were the incumbent directors immediately
    before the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where there is no approved
  rectification plan

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsections 174(2) and (3) and
  paragraph 179(1)(a), where a plan to rectify the non-compliance
  referred to in subsection 177(1) has not been approved by the
  Superintendent by the end of the forty-five day period referred to in
  that subsection, the board of directors shall, until their successors
  are elected or appointed, consist solely of the persons who were the
  incumbent directors immediately before the meeting at which the
  purported election or appointment referred to in that subsection
  occurred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors to call meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where subsection (1) or (1.1) applies, the board of
  directors referred to in that subsection shall without delay call a
  special meeting of members or shareholders to fill the vacancies,
  where paragraph 177(2)(a) applies, or elect a new board of directors,
  where subsection 177(1) or paragraph 177(2)(b) applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calling meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the directors fail to call a special meeting
  required by subsection (2), the meeting may be called by any person
  entitled to vote at that meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 178
- 1997, c. 15, s. 122
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to hold office

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director ceases to hold office

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the close of the annual meeting at which the
    director's term of office expires;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the director dies or resigns;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when the director becomes disqualified under
    section 170 or ineligible to hold office pursuant to subsection
    207(2);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} when the director is removed under section 180; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} when the director is removed from office under
    section 441.2.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of resignation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The resignation of a director of an association
  becomes effective at the time a written resignation is sent to the
  association by the director or at the time specified in the
  resignation, whichever is later.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 179
- 2001, c. 9, s. 277
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of director

- **[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  the members of an association may, by special resolution at a special
  meeting of members, remove any or all directors from office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where members of an association have the exclusive
  right to appoint or elect one or more directors, a director so
  appointed or elected may be removed only by those members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the holders of any class or series of shares of
  an association have the exclusive right to elect one or more
  directors, a director so elected may be removed only by a special
  resolution at a meeting of the shareholders of that class or series.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy by removal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A vacancy created by the removal of a director may be
  filled at the meeting of the members or shareholders at which the
  director is removed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of director

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} resigns,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    called for the purpose of removing the director from office, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} receives a notice or otherwise learns of a meeting
    of directors, members or shareholders at which another person is to
    be appointed or elected to fill the office of director, whether
    because of the director's resignation or removal or because the
    director's term of office has expired or is about to expire,

  is entitled to submit to the association a written statement giving
  the reasons for the resignation or the reasons why the director
  opposes any proposed action or resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement re disagreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a director resigns as a result of a
  disagreement with the other directors or the officers of an
  association, the director shall submit to the association and the
  Superintendent a written statement setting out the nature of the
  disagreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circulation of statement

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Forthwith on receipt of a director's
  statement referred to in subsection 181(1) relating to a matter
  referred to in paragraph 181(1)(b) or (c), or a director's statement
  referred to in subsection 181(2), an association shall send a copy
  thereof to the Superintendent, to each member and, if the director was
  elected by the holders of shares of a class, to those shareholders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An association is not required to comply with
  subsection (1) in respect of shareholders if the statement is included
  in or attached to a management proxy circular required by paragraph
  166.05(1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity for statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No association or person acting on its behalf incurs
  any liability by reason only of circulating a director's statement in
  compliance with subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 182
- 1997, c. 15, s. 123
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filling vacancy

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the by-laws of an
  association, a vacancy among the directors of the association is to be
  filled by an appointment or election by members only or by an
  appointment or election by persons having an exclusive right to
  appoint or elect one or more directors if the vacancy occurs among the
  directors appointed or elected by those persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where composition fails

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 188, where by reason of a
  vacancy the number of directors or the composition of the board of
  directors fails to meet any of the requirements of sections 169 and
  171, the directors who, pursuant to the by-laws, are empowered to fill
  that vacancy shall do so forthwith.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class vacancy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 188, the by-laws of an
  association may provide that, where a class of persons has an
  exclusive right to appoint or elect one or more directors and a
  vacancy occurs among those directors,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the remaining directors appointed or elected by
    those persons may fill the vacancy except one resulting from an
    increase in the number or the minimum or maximum number of directors
    who are to be appointed or elected by that class or from a failure
    to appoint or elect the number or minimum number of directors who
    are to be appointed or elected by that class;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there are no such remaining directors and, by
    reason of the vacancy, the number of directors or the composition of
    the board of directors fails to meet any of the requirements of
    sections 169 and 171, the other directors may fill that vacancy; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there are no such remaining directors and
    paragraph (b) does not apply, any person of that class of persons
    may call a meeting of the class for the purpose of filling the
    vacancy.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 183
- 2005, c. 54, s. 172
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-183-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 183"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unexpired term

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** Unless the by-laws
otherwise provide, a director appointed or elected to fill a vacancy
holds office for the unexpired term of the director's predecessor in
office.

#### [Meetings of the Board]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98406 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meetings required

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors shall meet at least four times during each financial year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place for meetings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The directors may meet at any place unless the
  by-laws provide otherwise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice for meetings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The notice for the meetings must be given as required
  by the by-laws.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 185
- 1997, c. 15, s. 124
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of meeting

- **[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  notice of a meeting of directors shall specify each matter referred to
  in section 202 that is to be dealt with at the meeting but, unless the
  by-laws otherwise provide, need not otherwise specify the purpose of
  or the business to be transacted at the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director may in any manner waive notice of a
  meeting of directors and the attendance of a director at a meeting of
  directors is a waiver of notice of that meeting except where the
  director attends the meeting for the express purpose of objecting to
  the transaction of any business on the grounds that the meeting is not
  lawfully called.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjourned meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notice of an adjourned meeting of directors is not
  required to be given if the time and place of the adjourned meeting
  was announced at the original meeting.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

- **[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 188, the number
  of directors referred to in subsection (2) constitutes a quorum at any
  meeting of directors or a committee of directors and, notwithstanding
  any vacancy among the directors, a quorum of directors may exercise
  all the powers of the directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The number of directors constituting a quorum at any
  meeting of directors or a committee of directors shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a majority of the minimum number of directors
    required by this Act for the board of directors or a committee of
    directors; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} such greater number of directors than the number
    calculated pursuant to paragraph (a) as may be established by the
    by-laws of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Director continues to be present

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director who is present at a meeting of directors
  or of a committee of directors but is not, in accordance with
  subsection 207(1), present at any particular time during the meeting
  is considered to be present for the purposes of this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 187
- 2005, c. 54, s. 173
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-187-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 187"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resident Canadian majority

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors of an association shall not transact business at a meeting
  of directors unless a majority of the directors present are resident
  Canadians.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the directors of an
  association may transact business at a meeting of directors without
  the required proportion of directors who are resident Canadians if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a director who is a resident Canadian unable to be
    present approves, in writing or by telephonic, electronic or other
    communications facilities, the business transacted at the meeting;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there would have been present the required
    proportion of directors who are resident Canadians had that director
    been present at the meeting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 188
- 2013, c. 33, s. 109
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic meeting

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the by-laws of an
  association, a meeting of directors or of a committee of directors may
  be held by means of such telephonic, electronic or other
  communications facilities as permit all persons participating in the
  meeting to communicate adequately with each other during the meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed present

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director participating in a meeting by any means
  referred to in subsection (1) is deemed for the purposes of this Act
  to be present at that meeting.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolution outside board meeting

- **[[189.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A resolution in writing signed by
  all the directors entitled to vote on that resolution at a meeting of
  directors is as valid as if it had been passed at a meeting of
  directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing directors' resolution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the resolution referred to in
  subsection (1) shall be kept with the minutes of the proceedings of
  the directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolution outside committee meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A resolution in writing signed by all the directors
  entitled to vote on that resolution at a meeting of a committee of
  directors, other than a resolution of the audit committee in carrying
  out its duties under subsection 199(3) or a resolution of the conduct
  review committee in carrying out its duties under subsection 200(3),
  is as valid as if it had been passed at a meeting of that committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing committee resolution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the resolution referred to in
  subsection (3) shall be kept with the minutes of the proceedings of
  that committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Unless a ballot is demanded, an entry in the minutes
  of a meeting that the chairperson declared a resolution to be carried
  or defeated is in the absence of evidence to the contrary proof of
  that fact without proof of the number or proportion of votes recorded
  in favour of or against the resolution.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 15, s. 125
- 2005, c. 54, s. 174
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-189.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 189.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissent of director

- **[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director of an association who is present at a meeting of directors or
  a committee of directors is deemed to have consented to any resolution
  passed or action taken at that meeting unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the director requests that the director's dissent
    be entered or the director's dissent is entered in the minutes of
    the meeting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the director sends a written dissent to the
    secretary of the meeting before the meeting is adjourned; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the director sends the director's dissent by
    registered mail or delivers it to the head office of the association
    immediately after the meeting is adjourned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of right to dissent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director of an association who votes for or
  consents to a resolution is not entitled to dissent under subsection
  (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissent of absent director

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director of an association who is not present at a
  meeting at which a resolution is passed or action taken is deemed to
  have consented thereto unless, within seven days after the director
  becomes aware of the resolution, the director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} causes the director's dissent to be placed with the
    minutes of the meeting; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sends the director's dissent by registered mail or
    delivers it to the head office of the association.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting required by Superintendent

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where in the opinion of the
  Superintendent it is necessary, the Superintendent may, by notice in
  writing, require an association to hold a meeting of directors of the
  association to consider the matters set out in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance of Superintendent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Superintendent may attend and be heard at a
  meeting referred to in subsection (1).

#### [By-laws]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98478 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws --- members

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  members of an association may by special resolution make, amend or
  repeal any by-law that regulates the business or affairs of the
  association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws --- directors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless this Act otherwise provides, the directors of
  an association may by special resolution make a by-law or amend a
  by-law of the association but only if the by-law or amendment is not
  contrary to any by-law made by the members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The directors shall submit a by-law, or an amendment
  to a by-law, that is made under subsection (2) to the members at the
  next meeting of members, and the members may, by special resolution,
  confirm or amend the by-law or amendment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where by-law not confirmed

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a by-law made by the directors pursuant to
  subsection (2) is not confirmed, with or without amendments, pursuant
  to subsection (3), the by-law is repealed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposal of by-law

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** A member may, in
accordance with section 152, make a proposal to make, amend or repeal a
by-law.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of by-law

- **[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless this Act otherwise provides,
  a by-law, or an amendment to or a repeal of a by-law, made by the
  members, is effective from the later of the date of the resolution
  made under subsection 192(1) and the date specified in the by-law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless this Act otherwise provides, a by-law, or an
  amendment to a by-law, made by the directors is effective from the
  later of the date that the by-law is made or amended by the directors
  and the date specified in the by-law, until it is confirmed, confirmed
  as amended under subsection 192(3) or repealed under subsection 192(4)
  and, where the by-law is confirmed, or confirmed as amended, it
  continues in effect in the form in which it was so confirmed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where by-law not approved

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a by-law, or an amendment to a by-law, made by
  the directors pursuant to 192(2) is not submitted by the directors at
  a meeting as required under subsection 192(3), the by-law or amendment
  ceases to be effective from the date of that meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New resolution by directors

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a by-law, or an amendment to a by-law, made by
  the directors pursuant to subsection 192(2) is repealed pursuant to
  subsection 192(4), or ceases to be effective pursuant to subsection
  (3), no subsequent resolution of directors to make or amend a by-law
  having substantially the same purpose or effect is effective until it
  is confirmed, or confirmed as amended, by the members.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws of former-Act association

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 196,
where a by-law of the former-Act association is in effect on the coming
into force of this section, the by-law continues in effect until amended
or repealed, unless it is contrary to a provision of this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws re remuneration

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  by-law of an association respecting the remuneration of the directors
  of the association, as directors, that is in effect on the coming into
  force of this Part ceases to have effect on the day on which the first
  annual meeting of the association is held following the coming into
  force of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Existing resolutions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the remuneration of directors of the former-Act
  association was, immediately prior to the coming into force of this
  Part, fixed by a resolution of the directors, that resolution
  continues to have effect, unless it is contrary to the provisions of
  this Act, until the first meeting of the members following the coming
  into force of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 192(3)
  and (4) and section 194

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 192(3) and (4) and section 194 apply in
  respect of a by-law referred to in this section as if it were a by-law
  made under section 192.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed by-laws

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  matter provided for in the incorporating instrument of the former-Act
  association on the coming into force of this Part that, under this
  Act, would be provided for in the by-laws of an association is deemed
  to be provided for in the by-laws of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-law prevails

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a by-law of the association made in accordance
  with sections 192 and 194 amends or repeals any matter referred to in
  subsection (1), the by-law prevails.

#### [Committees of the Board]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98513 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committees

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** The directors of an
association may appoint from their number, in addition to the committees
referred to in subsection 167(2), such other committees as they deem
necessary and, subject to section 202, delegate to those committees such
powers of the directors, and assign to those committees such duties, as
the directors consider appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit committee

- **[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  audit committee of an association shall consist of at least three
  directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} None of the members of the audit committee may be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} employees of the association or of a subsidiary of
    the association; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} officers of the association or of a subsidiary of
    the association who are involved in the day-to-day operation of the
    association or subsidiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of audit committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The audit committee of an association shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} review the annual statement of the association
    before the annual statement is approved by the directors;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} review such returns of the association as the
    Superintendent may specify;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} require the management of the association to
    implement and maintain appropriate internal control procedures;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} review, evaluate and approve those procedures;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} review such investments and transactions that could
    adversely affect the well-being of the association as the auditor or
    any officer of the association may bring to the attention of the
    committee;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} meet with the auditor to discuss the annual
    statement and the returns and transactions referred to in this
    subsection; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} meet with the chief internal auditor of the
    association, or the officer or employee of the association acting in
    a similar capacity, and with management of the association, to
    discuss the effectiveness of the internal control procedures
    established for the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of the annual statement and returns of an
  association that under this Act must be approved by the directors of
  the association, the audit committee of the association shall report
  thereon to the directors before the approval is given.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required meeting of directors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The audit committee of an association may call a
  meeting of the directors of the association to consider any matter of
  concern to the committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 199
- 1997, c. 15, s. 126
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct review committee

- **[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  conduct review committee of an association shall consist of at least
  three directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} None of the members of the conduct review committee
  may be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} employees of the association or of a subsidiary of
    the association; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} officers of the association or of a subsidiary of
    the association who are involved in the day-to-day operation of the
    association or subsidiary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of conduct review committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The conduct review committee of an association shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require the management of the association to
    establish procedures for complying with Part XII;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} review those procedures and their effectiveness in
    ensuring that the association is complying with Part XII; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} review the practices of the association to ensure
    that any transactions with related parties of the association that
    may have a material effect on the stability or solvency of the
    association are identified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Association report to Superintendent

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An association shall report to the Superintendent on
  the mandate and responsibilities of the conduct review committee and
  the procedures referred to in paragraph (3)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committee report to directors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} After each meeting of the conduct review committee of
  an association, the committee shall report to the directors of the
  association on matters reviewed by the committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors report to Superintendent

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Within ninety days after the end of each financial
  year, the directors of an association shall report to the
  Superintendent on what the conduct review committee did during the
  year in carrying out its responsibilities under subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 200
- 1997, c. 15, s. 127
- 2001, c. 9, s. 278
:::

#### [Directors and Officers --- Authority]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98558 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of officers

- **[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  directors of an association may, subject to the by-laws, designate the
  offices of the association, appoint officers thereto, specify the
  duties of those officers and delegate to them powers, subject to
  section 202, to manage the business and affairs of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors as officers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 171, a director of an association
  may be appointed to any office of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Two or more offices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Two or more offices of an association may be held by
  the same person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits on power to delegate

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** The directors of an
association may not delegate any of the following powers, namely, the
power to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} submit to the members or shareholders a question or
  matter requiring their approval;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} admit members;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} fill a vacancy among the directors, on a committee of
  directors or in the office of auditor, or appoint additional
  directors;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} issue or cause to be issued securities, including an
  issue of shares of a series that is authorized in accordance with
  section 71, except in accordance with any authorization made by the
  directors;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} declare a dividend on membership shares or shares, a
  payment of a bonus on any deposit or a patronage allocation;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} authorize the redemption or other acquisition by the
  association pursuant to section 79 of membership shares or shares
  issued by the association;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} authorize the payment of a commission on a share
  issue;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} approve a management proxy circular;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} except as provided in this Act, approve the annual
  statement of the association and any other financial statements issued
  by the association;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} expel members; or

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} adopt or amend by-laws.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 202
- 1997, c. 15, s. 128
- 2005, c. 54, s. 175
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration of directors, officers and
employees

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section and the
  by-laws, the directors of an association may fix the remuneration of
  the directors, officers and employees of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-law required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No remuneration shall be paid to a director as
  director until a by-law fixing the aggregate of all amounts that may
  be paid to all directors in respect of directors' remuneration during
  a fixed period of time has been confirmed by special resolution of the
  members.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 203
- 1994, c. 26, s. 26
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of acts

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  act of a director or an officer of an association is valid
  notwithstanding a defect in the director's qualification or an
  irregularity in the director's election or in the appointment of the
  director or officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An act of the board of directors of an association is
  valid notwithstanding a defect in the composition of the board or an
  irregularity in the election of the board or in the appointment of a
  member of the board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to attend meetings

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** A director of an
association is entitled to attend and to be heard at every meeting of
members or shareholders of the association.

#### [Conflicts of Interest]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98595 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of interest

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director or officer of an association shall disclose to the
  association, in writing or by requesting to have it entered in the
  minutes of a meeting of directors or a meeting of a committee of
  directors, the nature and extent of any interest they have in a
  material contract or material transaction with the association,
  whether entered into or proposed, if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are a party to the contract or transaction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are a director or officer of a party, other than a
    member, to the contract or transaction or a person acting in a
    similar capacity; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have a material interest in a party to the contract
    or transaction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure --- director

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure shall be made in the case of a
  director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the meeting of directors, or of a committee of
    directors, at which the proposed contract or transaction is first
    considered;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if at the time of the meeting referred to in
    paragraph (a) the director was not interested in the proposed
    contract or transaction, at the first one after they become
    interested in it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the director becomes interested after a contract
    or transaction is entered into, at the first one after they become
    interested; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if a person who is interested in a contract or
    transaction becomes a director, at the first one after they become a
    director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure --- officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure shall be made in the case of an
  officer who is not a director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately after they become aware that the
    contract, transaction, proposed contract or proposed transaction is
    to be considered or has been considered at a meeting of directors or
    of a committee of directors;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if they become interested after the contract or
    transaction is entered into, immediately after they become
    interested; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a person who is interested in a contract or
    transaction becomes an officer, immediately after they become an
    officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure --- contract not
  requiring approval

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the material contract or material transaction,
  whether entered into or proposed, is one that in the ordinary course
  of the association's business would not require approval by the
  directors or shareholders, the director or officer shall disclose to
  the association, in writing or by requesting to have it entered in the
  minutes of a meeting of directors or of a committee of directors, the
  nature and extent of their interest immediately after they become
  aware of the contract or transaction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 206
- 2005, c. 54, s. 176
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Director to abstain

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director who is required to make a disclosure under subsection 206(1)
  shall not be present at any meeting of directors, or of a committee of
  directors, while the contract or transaction is being considered or
  vote on any resolution to approve it unless the contract or
  transaction

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} relates primarily to their remuneration as a
    director, officer, employee or agent of the association, an entity
    controlled by the association or an entity in which the association
    has a substantial investment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is for indemnity under section 216 or insurance
    under section 217; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is with an affiliate of the association.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ineligibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any director who knowingly contravenes subsection (1)
  ceases to hold office as director and is not eligible, for a period of
  five years after the date on which the contravention occurred, for
  election or appointment as a director of any financial institution
  that is incorporated or formed by or under an Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of acts

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An act of the board of directors of an association or
  of a committee of the board of directors is not invalid because a
  person acting as a director had ceased under subsection (2) to hold
  office as a director.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 207
- 1997, c. 15, s. 129
- 2005, c. 54, s. 177
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General notice

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of subsection 206(1), a general notice to the directors
  declaring that a director or officer is to be regarded as interested
  for any of the following reasons in a contract or transaction entered
  into with a party is a sufficient declaration of interest in relation
  to any contract or transaction with that party:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the director or officer is a director or officer of
    a party referred to in paragraph 206(1)(b) or (c) or a person acting
    in a similar capacity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the director or officer has a material interest in
    the party; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there has been a material change in the nature of
    the director's or officer's interest in the party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to disclosures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members or shareholders of the association may
  examine the portions of any minutes of meetings of directors or
  committees of directors that contain disclosures under subsection
  206(1), or the portions of any other documents that contain those
  disclosures, during the usual business hours of the association.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 208
- 2005, c. 54, s. 178
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-208-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 208"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Avoidance standards

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  contract or transaction for which disclosure is required under
  subsection 206(1) is not invalid and a director or officer is not
  accountable to the association or its members or shareholders for any
  profit realized from it by reason only of the director's or officer's
  interest in the contract or transaction or the fact that the director
  was present or was counted to determine whether a quorum existed at
  the meeting of directors, or of a committee of directors, that
  considered it if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the director or officer disclosed their interest in
    accordance with section 206 and subsection 208(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the directors approved the contract or transaction;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the contract or transaction was reasonable and fair
    to the association at the time that it was approved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirmation by members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Even if the conditions set out in subsection (1) are
  not met, a director or officer acting honestly and in good faith is
  not accountable to the association or its members or shareholders for
  any profit realized from a contract or transaction for which
  disclosure was required and the contract or transaction is not invalid
  by reason only of the director's or officer's interest in it if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the contract or transaction is approved or
    confirmed by special resolution at a meeting of members;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disclosure of the interest was made to the members
    in a manner sufficient to indicate its nature before the contract or
    transaction was approved or confirmed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the contract or transaction was reasonable and fair
    to the association at the time that it was approved or confirmed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 209
- 2005, c. 54, s. 178
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may set aside or require accounting

**[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** If a director or
officer of an association fails to comply with any of sections 206 to
209, a court, on application of the association or any of its members or
shareholders, may set aside the contract or transaction on any terms
that the court thinks fit and may require the director or officer to
account to the association for any profit or gain realized on it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 210
- 2005, c. 54, s. 178
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-210-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 210"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Liability, Exculpation and Indemnification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98656 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Director liability

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Directors of an association who vote
  for or consent to a resolution of the directors authorizing the issue
  of a membership share or a share contrary to subsection 74(1) or the
  issue of subordinated indebtedness contrary to section 87 for a
  consideration other than money are jointly and severally, or
  solidarily, liable to the association to make good any amount by which
  the consideration received is less than the fair equivalent of the
  money that the association would have received if the membership share
  or share or subordinated indebtedness had been issued for money on the
  date of the resolution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Directors of an association who vote for or consent
  to a resolution of the directors authorizing any of the following are
  jointly and severally, or solidarily, liable to restore to the
  association any amounts so distributed or paid and not otherwise
  recovered by the association and any amounts in relation to any loss
  suffered by the association:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a redemption or purchase of membership shares or
    shares contrary to section 79;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reduction of capital contrary to section 82;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a payment of a dividend contrary to section 86;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a payment of an indemnity contrary to section 216;
    or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any transaction contrary to Part XII.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 211
- 2005, c. 54, s. 179(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contribution

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director who has satisfied a judgment in relation to the director's
  liability under section 211 is entitled to contribution from the other
  directors who voted for or consented to the unlawful act on which the
  judgment was founded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director who is liable under section 211 is
  entitled to apply to a court for an order compelling a member,
  shareholder or other person to pay or deliver to the director

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any money or property that was paid or distributed
    to the member, shareholder or other person contrary to section 79,
    82, 86 or 216; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to the value of the loss suffered
    by the association as a result of any transaction contrary to Part
    XII.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an application is made to a court under
  subsection (2), the court may, where it is satisfied that it is
  equitable to do so,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order a member, shareholder or other person to pay
    or deliver to a director any money or property that was paid or
    distributed to the member, shareholder or other person contrary to
    section 79, 82, 86 or 216 or any amount referred to in paragraph
    (2)(b);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order an association to return or issue membership
    shares or shares to a member or shareholder from whom the
    association has purchased, redeemed or otherwise acquired membership
    shares or shares; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make any further order it thinks fit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** An action to enforce a
liability imposed by section 211 may not be commenced after two years
from the date of the resolution authorizing the action complained of.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors liable for wages

- **[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  the directors of an association are jointly and severally, or
  solidarily, liable to each employee of the association for all debts
  not exceeding six months wages payable to the employee for services
  performed for the association while they are directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions precedent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director is not liable under subsection (1) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the association has been sued for the debt within
    six months after it has become due and execution has been returned
    unsatisfied in whole or in part;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the association has commenced liquidation and
    dissolution proceedings or has been dissolved and a claim for the
    debt has been proven within six months after the earlier of the date
    of commencement of the liquidation and dissolution proceedings and
    the date of dissolution; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a winding-up order has been issued in respect of
    the association under the [Winding-up and Restructuring
    Act](/eng/acts/W-11) and a claim for the debt has been allowed or
    proven within six months after the issue of the winding-up order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A director is not liable under subsection (1) unless
  the director is sued for a debt referred to in that subsection while a
  director or within two years after the director has ceased to be a
  director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount due after execution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where execution referred to in paragraph (2)(a) has
  issued, the amount recoverable from a director is the amount remaining
  unsatisfied after execution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subrogation of director

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a director of an association pays a debt
  referred to in subsection (1) that is proven in liquidation and
  dissolution or winding-up proceedings, the director is entitled to any
  preference that the employee would have been entitled to and, where a
  judgment has been obtained, the director is entitled to an assignment
  of the judgment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contribution entitlement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A director of an association who has satisfied a
  claim under this section is entitled to a contribution from the other
  directors of the association who are liable for the claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 214
- 1996, c. 6, s. 167
- 2005, c. 54, s. 180(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence --- due diligence

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  director, officer or employee of an association is not liable under
  section 211 or 214 or subsection 430(1) and has fulfilled their duty
  under subsection 168(2) if they exercised the care, diligence and
  skill that a reasonably prudent person would have exercised in
  comparable circumstances, including reliance in good faith on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the association that were
    represented to them by an officer of the association or in a written
    report of the auditor of the association fairly to reflect the
    financial condition of the association; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report of a person whose profession lends
    credibility to a statement made by them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence --- good faith

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director or officer of an association has fulfilled
  their duty under subsection 168(1) if they relied in good faith on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} financial statements of the association that were
    represented to them by an officer of the association or in a written
    report of the auditor of the association fairly to reflect the
    financial condition of the association; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a report of a person whose profession lends
    credibility to a statement made by them.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 215
- 2001, c. 9, s. 279
- 2005, c. 54, s. 181
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 215"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indemnification

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  association may indemnify a director or officer of the association, a
  former director or officer of the association or another person who
  acts or acted, at the association's request, as a director or officer
  of or in a similar capacity for another entity against all costs,
  charges and expenses, including an amount paid to settle an action or
  satisfy a judgment, reasonably incurred by them in respect of any
  civil, criminal, administrative, investigative or other proceeding in
  which they are involved because of that association with the
  association or other entity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advances

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An association may advance amounts to the director,
  officer or other person for the costs, charges and expenses of a
  proceeding referred to in subsection (1). They shall repay the amounts
  if they do not fulfil the conditions set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No indemnification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An association may not indemnify a person under
  subsection (1) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person acted honestly and in good faith with a
    view to the best interests of, as the case may be, the association
    or the other entity for which they acted at the association's
    request as a director or officer or in a similar capacity; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a criminal or administrative action
    or proceeding that is enforced by a monetary penalty, the person had
    reasonable grounds for believing that their conduct was lawful.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indemnification --- derivative actions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An association may with the approval of a court
  indemnify a person referred to in subsection (1) or advance amounts to
  them under subsection (2) --- in respect of an action by or on behalf
  of the association or other entity to procure a judgment in its favour
  to which the person is made a party because of the association
  referred to in subsection (1) with the association or other entity ---
  against all costs, charges and expenses reasonably incurred by them in
  connection with that action if they fulfil the conditions set out in
  subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to indemnity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person referred to in that
  subsection is entitled to be indemnified by the association in respect
  of all costs, charges and expenses reasonably incurred by them in
  connection with the defence of any civil, criminal, administrative,
  investigative or other proceeding to which the person is subject
  because of their association referred to in subsection (1) with the
  association or other entity described in that subsection if the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was not judged by the court or other competent
    authority to have committed any fault or omitted to do anything that
    they ought to have done; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fulfils the conditions set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Heirs and personal representatives

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An association may, to the extent referred to in
  subsections (1) to (5) in respect of the person, indemnify the heirs
  or personal representatives of any person whom the association may
  indemnify under those subsections.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 216
- 2001, c. 9, s. 280(F)
- 2005, c. 54, s. 181
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directors' and officers' insurance

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** An association may
purchase and maintain insurance for the benefit of any person referred
to in section 216 against any liability incurred by the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the capacity of a director or an officer of the
  association, except where the liability relates to a failure to act
  honestly and in good faith with a view to the best interests of the
  association; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the capacity of a director or officer of another
  entity or while acting in a similar capacity for another entity, if
  they act or acted in that capacity at the association's request,
  except if the liability relates to a failure to act honestly and in
  good faith with a view to the best interests of the entity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 217
- 2005, c. 54, s. 182
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court for indemnification

- **[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  association or a person referred to in section 216 may apply to a
  court for an order approving an indemnity under that section and the
  court may so order and make any further order it thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Superintendent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An applicant under subsection (1) shall give the
  Superintendent written notice of the application and the
  Superintendent is entitled to appear and to be heard at the hearing of
  the application in person or by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On an application under subsection (1), the court may
  order notice to be given to any interested person and that person is
  entitled to appear and to be heard in person or by counsel at the
  hearing of the application.

### [Fundamental Changes]{.HTitleText2} {#h-98744 .Subheading}

#### [Amendments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-98745 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporating instrument

**[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** On the application of
an association duly authorized by special resolution of the members, the
Minister may approve a proposal to add, change or remove any provision
that is permitted by this Act to be set out in the association's
incorporating instrument.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 219
- 2001, c. 9, s. 281
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Letters patent to amend

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of an application referred to in section 219, the Minister may
  issue letters patent to effect the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of letters patent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Letters patent issued pursuant to subsection (1)
  become effective on the day stated in the letters patent.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 48, s. 220
- 2001, c. 9, s. 282
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  members of an association may, by special resolution, make, amend or
  repeal any by-laws, in the manner set out in subsection (2) and
  sections 222 to 225, to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} change the maximum number, if any, of shares of any
    class that the association is authorized to issue;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} create new classes of shares;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} change the designation of any or all of the
    association's shares, and add, change or remove any rights,
    privileges, restrictions and conditions, including rights to accrued
    dividends, in respect of any or all of the association's shares,
    whether issued or unissued;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} change the shares of any class or series, whether
    issued or unissued, into a different number of shares of the same
    class or series or into the same or a different number of shares of
    other classes or series;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} divide a class of shares, whether issued or
    unissued, into series and fix the maximum number of shares, if any,
    in each series and the rights, privileges, restrictions and
    conditions attached thereto;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} authorize the directors to divide any class of
